## 2026-09-19 — TDA T1: the gateway transform, and the matching rule the plan lacked

- `provider/gateway/tda.ts` (new): `applyTemporaryDataAcquisition(body, set, turn)`. The gateway WITHHOLDS, never supplies — only this layer sees the true outgoing body, and re-attaching from here would mean holding image bytes (plan §0.1). Four rules: withhold a released/expired item → a pointer reusing the dropped-result grammar (`REPLAY_DELIVERED_MARKER` **imported**, not copied, so `isReplayReduced` recognises both) · keep a held item untouched · **NEVER BLANK** (no address ⇒ no replacement — the invariant the `recall` `keep` defect bought) · no-op on an empty set or an unparsable body, returning the INPUT STRING rather than a re-serialisation, because that byte-identity is what makes T2's flag-off control meaningful.
- **Decision the plan did not have (now §0.2.1): matching is CONTENT-ADDRESSED.** An `image_url` entry is `{type, image_url:{url}}` — the body carries NO part id, so the payload itself is the only handle: `TdaHeld.digest` + an exported `payloadDigest` (ONE implementation, shared with the acquirer; two would drift invisibly and surface as an item that can never be released). The `[Image N]` ordinal was rejected — it couples this layer to the open numbering decision (§4.1) and breaks for label-less items (a CUA frame, a document, a stored source). `TdaHeld.reader` is declared by the runtime: the pointer must not name a reader it cannot promise, which is exactly the lie the placeholder shipped once.
- Media shape grounded without guessing: `test/provider/deepseek-image.test.ts` captures a REAL request body from the installed SDK and asserts `content[]` holds `{type:"image_url", image_url:{url:"data:image/webp;base64,…"}}`. The T1 fixture reproduces that.
- **My own error, corrected in place:** I attributed the absence of media in `raw-wire/` to AGE (25 image parts ~5.3 h older than the earliest capture) and wrote it into the test doc, plan §0.6-5 and a commit BEFORE reading `_progress_log.md`. The record refutes it: `@ai-sdk/deepseek@3.0.26`'s converter was text-only — non-text parts went to `warnings`, never serialized («183 raw-wire bodies scanned: zero image parts») — fixed by the 3.0.48 bump. **Two timestamps are not a cause.** This is the third instance of the reflex already named in memory (unexplained state → plausible external cause), and the instrument was one read away.
- Oracle [Exact]: `bun typecheck` exit 0 · `test/provider/gateway-tda.test.ts` **8 pass / 0 fail / 25 expect** — withhold · keep · blank-guard · unparsable no-op · untouched-body byte-identity · digest stability across mime wrappers.
- Cost shape: flag-OFF is exactly ZERO (short-circuit returns the input); with something released it is one `JSON.parse` + `JSON.stringify` (the shape the reasoning rewrite already pays) plus one sha256 per `data:` payload. The NUMBER belongs to T2, on the real hot path.
- Residual: T2 (wire into `wrapFetch` beside `rewriteReasoningContent`; flag-off byte-identity) · T3 (the set's write path: acquire/hold/release/expire) · T4 (the flag, default OFF, every limit a number in `gateway.jsonc`) · T5 (sandbox: separate build, `opencode/big-pickle`, **must carry an image inside the run**) · T6–T8.

## 2026-09-19 — TDA T2: the transform is ON THE WIRE; the absence of the header IS the flag

- `adaptive-client.ts`: the transform runs inside `wrapFetch`, AFTER `rewriteReasoningContent` (so it sees the body that actually goes out), gated only on the runtime declaring a set in `x-opencode-tda`. The header is CONSUMED and deleted like the credential headers — it has been folded into the body, and an instruction the provider can read is noise at best.
- `tda.ts` — `parseTdaHeader(value)`: ONE pure parser, and every failure answers `undefined` (no header · malformed JSON · wrong shape · nothing held · items failing the shape guard), i.e. "nothing is held". On the hot path of every request an unreadable instruction must neither throw nor half-apply. `turn` travels WITH the set: only the runtime knows which turn the outgoing request belongs to.
- **Plan refinement — it removes a surface:** the GATEWAY needs no flag. It withholds only when handed a set, so `gateway.tda.enabled` belongs to the RUNTIME's config and T4 becomes "the runtime reads the flag and sends the header": one authority for the switch instead of two, and the zero-cost short-circuit holds whenever nothing is held.
- Oracle [Exact]: `bun typecheck` exit 0 · `test/provider/gateway-tda-wire.test.ts` **5 pass / 0 fail / 17 expect** — withheld on the wire with the pointer in place and its neighbours untouched · byte-identical with no header · a held item untouched · a body carrying a different payload untouched · five malformed headers degrade to nothing · the instruction is not forwarded upstream (with a forwarded header as control) · `test/provider/gateway-tda.test.ts` 8/0/25 (T1 intact) · `test/provider/adaptive-client.test.ts` 4/0/35 (the gateway sibling, unchanged — the wiring broke nothing).
- Method note: the wire suite deliberately uses a model that does NOT match `/z-ai\/|glm|deepseek/i`, because that regex routes the body through `rewriteReasoningContent`, which re-serialises the JSON and would mask a real difference. Isolating the variable is what makes "byte-identical" mean anything.
- Residual: T3 (the set's write path — acquire/hold/release/expire, every §0.9 limit a number in config) · T4 (the runtime flag, default OFF) · T5 (sandbox: separate build, `opencode/big-pickle`, **must carry an image INSIDE the run**) · T6–T8.

## 2026-09-19 — TDA T3a: the set's rules, pure and pinned; and two groundings that bound the feature

- `session/acquired-item.ts` (new, **PURE**): `acquiredItem()` computes the digest from the payload url — so no call site can invent a digest the matcher would not compute, and an item that can never be matched is an item that can never be released · `release()` withholds AT ONCE and does NOT rewrite the span · `tdaHeaderValue()` carries the items plus the turn they are judged at.
- **`isWithheld` is exported from the CONTRACT module and BOTH sides call it.** The runtime decides what to send and the gateway decides what to replace, so they must agree BY CONSTRUCTION, not by review: two implementations would disagree invisibly.
- **Release and expiry are TWO facts, now two fields.** The plan's own words are "a released or expired item"; writing a release as "the expiry moved into the past" would blur a decision into a coincidence. `TdaHeld.released?: boolean` added; `isWithheld = released || expiresAtTurn < turn`.
- Turn source, settled by grounding: **a turn is a USER PROMPT**, counted from the session's own event log — `session_entry.type = 'user'`, indexed by `(session_id, type)` — so the count is DERIVED, survives a restart, and costs no per-turn write.
- **BOUND 1 — the header's reach.** `x-opencode-*` go EXCLUSIVELY to opencode-owned providers (`session/llm.ts:959-966`, three-layer contract 2026-09-08: «third-party providers react badly to foreign namespaced headers»). So TDA is INERT on deepseek-direct / novita / openrouter — a boundary, not a defect to route around — and it makes the sandbox's `opencode/big-pickle` choice load-bearing for OBSERVATION, not only for cost.
- **BOUND 2 — the plan's own claim was half-true.** `x-opencode-has-attachments` occurs exactly ONCE in src and it is the READER; there is no writer, so `hasAttachments` is permanently false. "`x-opencode-tda`, beside has-attachments" therefore describes a surface that must be CREATED, not joined. Plan §0.3 corrected.
- **Tooling falsifier, third instance:** `multiedit` reported «No changes to apply: oldString and newString are identical» while having APPLIED three of four edits — including a mangled body (`!releaseal(item)` / `released.set(...)`) that broke the transform. It was caught because the file was RE-READ before the repair and the next oracle would have failed. **Believe the file, not the report** — already in memory, and still worth re-learning.
- Oracle [Exact]: `bun typecheck` exit 0 · `test/session/acquired-item.test.ts` **5 pass / 0 fail / 23 expect** · `test/provider/gateway-tda.test.ts` 9/0/26 (+1: a released item is withheld though its span has not passed) · `test/provider/gateway-tda-wire.test.ts` 5/0/17.
- Note: LSP flagged `session/llm.ts:604` «Type instantiation is excessively deep and possibly infinite» while the session folder was touched; `tsgo --noEmit` exits 0, so it is plugin strictness (the class of the ten floating `slog.*`), not a build error — recorded, not ignored.
- Residual: **T3b** — the `acquired_item` table (DDL in `storage/db.ts` + the drizzle schema; the DDL is idempotent at open, so existing DBs self-heal), the turn counter, and the header at the send site. Then T4 (the runtime flag, default OFF), T5 (sandbox), T6–T8.

## 2026-09-19 — TDA §0.6: the sandbox model — the owner's requirement was right, the route was not

- Owner: «давай попробуем эту [openrouter/thinkingmachines/inkling:free], big-pickle не понимает картинок, не получится все проверить». The requirement is CORRECT and now measured: `opencode/big-pickle` is absent from the 88 vision-capable models (instrument kept: `.opencode/opencode-models-probe.mjs`).
- The ROUTE was the problem, not the model: `x-opencode-*` reach ONLY `providerID.startsWith("opencode")` (§0.3, three-layer contract 2026-09-08), so on an OpenRouter model the TDA instruction never leaves the process, the gateway receives no set, and the sandbox would read a clean no-op as success — «works» out of silence.
- Both constraints hold at once, for FREE: `opencode` hosts 107 models, 88 with image input, SEVEN at 0/0. Owner ruling: **`opencode/qwen3.6-plus-free`** (`text+image+video`, `attachment: true`, 0/0). Same-class alternatives: `kimi-k2.5-free`, `mimo-v2.5-free`, `mimo-v2-omni-free`, `x-preview-f-free`, `muse-spark-1.2/1.3-contributor-free`.
- Catalog caveat worth keeping: `minimax-m3-free` declares `text+image+video` with `attachment: false` — image INPUT and attachment ACCEPTANCE are different flags, and only the second makes an item transportable.
- Confirmed while grounding: `mod.ts:80` installs the wrapped fetch as a PROCESS GLOBAL (`globalThis.__gatewayFetch`), so the TDA transform runs on every provider — the header's reach is the only gate, not the wiring.

## 2026-09-19 — TDA T3b: the set became a ROW; and four cases of the tool lying

- `acquired_item` (DDL in `storage/db.ts` + the drizzle declaration) and `session/acquired-item-store.ts`: reads/writes, the turn counter, and ONE entry point (`tdaHeaderFor`).
- **The schema oracle reads the ARTIFACT.** Two descriptions of one table — the `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` DDL and the drizzle declaration — and nothing else compares them, so the test asserts `PRAGMA table_info('acquired_item')` on a fresh project DB, all ten columns. The write-path rule, applied to a schema.
- **A turn is a user prompt**, counted from `session_entry` (indexed by `(session_id, type)`): derived, restart-proof. `tdaHeaderFor` reads the items FIRST and counts turns ONLY when there are items — so the feature costs nothing until something is actually acquired, mirroring the transform's own short circuit on the far side of the wire.
- **The `PartID` brand was carried through the whole chain** (contract → constructor → store → tests) with no cast anywhere in the write path: an item's address IS a part's address, and the brand stops a session or message id from being accepted where a part id is meant. `acquiredItem` brands it once, in the single constructor — earlier the plan's type had `id: string` and the column had a brand, which is how the chain surfaced it.
- A semantic refinement fell out of the brand: an EMPTY address can no longer be built by the runtime, so rule 3's case is reachable only OFF THE WIRE — which is exactly why the guard belongs on the transform's side. Recorded in that test.
- **The tool lied four times in this task, and for the first time in the OPPOSITE direction:** `multiedit` reported «Could not find oldString» while having applied the edits. So the report is evidence in NEITHER direction — only the file is. Separately, my own error: an edit dropped the `session_id` line from the fixture (my oldString included it, my newString did not) → `NOT NULL constraint failed: session_entry.session_id`, i.e. the oracle caught what my eyes did not.
- Oracle [Exact]: `bun typecheck` exit 0 · `acquired-item-store` **3/0/13** · `acquired-item` 5/0/23 · `gateway-tda` 9/0/26 · `gateway-tda-wire` 5/0/17.
- Residual: **T3c** — the header at the send site in `llm.ts`, inside the `providerID.startsWith("opencode")` block. Then T4 (the runtime flag, default OFF), T5 (the sandbox on `qwen3.6-plus-free`, with an image INSIDE the run), T6–T8.

## 2026-09-19 — TDA T3c: the instruction rides only where the contract allows, and that boundary is PINNED

- `llm.ts`: `...tdaHeaders(input.model.providerID, tdaHeaderFor(input.sessionID))` INSIDE the existing `providerID.startsWith("opencode")` block. The send surface was CREATED, not joined — `x-opencode-has-attachments` has a reader and no writer anywhere.
- The decision moved into a PURE `tdaHeaders(providerID, value)` beside the lifecycle: `{}` when there is nothing to send, `{}` for every third-party provider, the header only for `opencode`/`opencode-go`. Both boundaries are therefore PINNED in `acquired-item.test.ts` (openrouter, deepseek, novita-ai, openai each send nothing) rather than described in a comment — the contract's edge became an assertion.
- **Branding decided AT THE SEAM, by the compiler:** the transport layer holds `input.sessionID` as a PLAIN STRING, so the read path (`tdaHeaderFor`) accepts one and brands it ONCE at the store boundary; the write path (`acquire`/`releaseItem`) stays branded, because a wrongly-shaped id there would write a row nobody could ever find again. Found because `tsgo` refused the call, not because anyone noticed.
- Oracle [Exact]: `bun typecheck` exit 0 · `acquired-item` **6/0/30** (+1 case, +7 assertions) · `acquired-item-store` 3/0/13 · `gateway-tda` 9/0/26 · `gateway-tda-wire` 5/0/17.
- Also corrected in the plan: T5's row still named `opencode/big-pickle` — a plan line lagging its own ruling. Now `opencode/qwen3.6-plus-free`.
- **Residual:** the WIRING LINE is not unit-tested — driving a whole request through `llm.ts` would test the pipeline rather than the decision. T5's sandbox observes it on the wire, which is the oracle the plan names for it.
- Tooling, again: two edits reported «Could not find oldString»; one had genuinely not applied because the file's import line is NOT what I remembered writing. Reading the file settled it in one call — the memory of my own write settled nothing.

## 2026-09-19 — TDA T4: the switch, four limits as numbers, and five failures attributed rather than waved away

- `config.ts`: `gateway.tda` declared beside `gateway.logDir` — `enabled` (off), `holdTurns`, `maxItems`, `maxItemBytes`, `maxHeldTokens`, `priceMargin`. Every §0.9 limit is a number in a FILE, not a constant in a function.
- The decision stayed PURE: `tdaHeaders(providerID, value, enabled)` carries BOTH gates, so neither can be forgotten at a call site; the send site is a plain spread reading `cfg.gateway?.tda?.enabled === true` — the same `cfg` the function already holds (`llm.ts:503`), so no second config path was invented.
- **Three gates, three assertions** in `acquired-item.test.ts`: switch OFF ⇒ nothing even for `opencode` with a live set (§0.9-4 — a pipeline that can hold a gigabyte must be something you turned ON, and a DEFAULT cannot be forgotten the way a resolution can) · third party ⇒ nothing even with the switch on · nothing acquired ⇒ nothing.
- **Enforcement of the four limits lands with the ACQUIRER**, and that is a decision rather than a deferral: declaring a limit before there is anything that could be refused is honest; enforcing it where nothing can yet be acquired would be a guard with no subject.
- Oracle [Exact]: `bun typecheck` exit 0 · `acquired-item` **6/0/31** · `acquired-item-store` 3/0/13 · `gateway-tda` 9/0/26 · `gateway-tda-wire` 5/0/17.
- `bun test test/config/` — the proportional surface for the schema change — **171 pass / 5 fail**, and NOT ONE of the five is this change (additive optional field; the failures are about `config.plugin`):
  1. `config.test.ts` «installs dependencies in writable OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR» — **TIMEOUT at 6079 ms** against bun's 5000 ms default inside a 222 s run. The signature of a timeout, not of logic.
  2..5. `tui.test.ts` ×4 — an EXTRA plugin entry `file:///…/.opencode/plugins/tui-smoke.tsx` appears in the merged TUI config. Mechanism: `Global.Path.home` is the WORKTREE (AGENTS.md), so the "global" config resolves to this repo's own `.opencode/` and a LOCAL, gitignored plugin left by an earlier TUI smoke leaks into every test that merges global config. An environment artifact, not a code defect — recorded here so the next cycle does not re-derive it, and so it is not silently waved away as "pre-existing".
- Residual: **T5** — the sandbox: a separate build, `opencode/qwen3.6-plus-free` (vision, free, and a route the instruction can actually travel), an image INSIDE the run, and the per-request bodies as the observation surface.

## 2026-09-19 — TDA T3d: compaction is a HARD release trigger (owner ruling)

- Owner: «если TDA больше чем на один ход, то пусть они двигаюся по цепочке сообщений чтобы быть закешированными… но здесь один нюанс если мы залезаем на компакт, мы должны их тут же отпустить и информировать модель». Recorded as plan §0.9.1 — and it SUPERSEDES §2.2's rationale (the tail block is right about the PREFIX and wrong about the BILL: a tail block is re-sent every turn at full input price, while a payload inside the chain costs `cache_read` — 1/50 on our models).
- **The transform needed no change, and that is not luck:** it matches by `digest` and replaces the payload IN PLACE, so placement (prefix or tail) is invisible to it. The placement decision turned out to be SEPARATE from the withholding mechanism — nothing built (T1/T2, T3b) required a single line.
- **`releaseAll(sessionID)`** in the store and the hook in `prompt.ts`: the fold decision is computed ONCE into `foldChoice`, and anything but `defer` releases everything BEFORE the fold runs. Two decisions — the fold and the release — are made in ONE place, so they cannot disagree.
- The release is **not an erasure**: spans untouched, items still there, which is what lets the model bring them back by the id the pointer prints. Expected behaviour, in the owner's words: «приведет дела в порядок, сделает компакт и захватит файлы снова».
- Oracle [Exact]: `bun typecheck` exit 0 · `test/session/acquired-item-store.test.ts` **4 pass / 0 fail / 17 expect** (+1 case: held by span, released by the fold anyway; spans preserved; items still present).
- **My error, repaired immediately:** a plan edit replaced the T4 row with the T3d row, deleting T4's record. Restored from the text — with the same lesson the whole evening keeps teaching: an `oldString` chosen as "the line I want to replace" must contain EVERYTHING that has to survive.
- Residual: the `prompt.ts` hook is not unit-tested (that is the pipeline, not the decision) — it is observed on the wire in T5. Next: T5 — a separate build, `opencode/qwen3.6-plus-free`, an image INSIDE the run.

## 2026-09-19 — TDA T5: the sandbox WORKS and the provider closed the door (a finding, not a failure)

- The sandbox comes up: `experiments/2026-09-19_tda-sandbox/_run.cmd` runs the SOURCE tree with its own `OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR` and its own `OPENCODE_DB`, and the agent started on the intended model (`> build_mode · mimo-v2.5-free`).
- **Two corrections by measurement, both worth keeping:**
  1. `src/index.ts <path> run "…"` → **exit 1 + usage**. The project path is accepted as a positional for the TUI but NOT before a subcommand, and the launcher already `cd`s into the experiment — so `_run.cmd run "…"` is the working form.
  2. `opencode/qwen3.6-plus-free` — the first choice, taken from the CATALOG — is **absent from the list the runtime resolves**. `_run.cmd models` (exit 0, 810+ lines) serves eight free `opencode/*` models and that is not one of them. **Durable lesson: the catalog is models.dev, not the list of what can be called**; the authoritative list is `opencode models` inside the configuration the run will use. The sandbox moved to `opencode/mimo-v2.5-free` (served · text+image+audio+video · attachment:true · 0/0).
- **BLOCKER found by running it:** `Error from provider (Console): OpenCode's free tier can only be used from within OpenCode`. The free `opencode/*` tier is gated by CLIENT, and this dev build is not counted as one. So phase 1 (the body carrying an image) was NOT captured and `per-request/` does not exist. This is not a build error and not a code error: config, launcher, isolation and model resolution were all confirmed working.
- **The fork (the owner's decision — the repository cannot settle it):** either the sandbox reaches a provider that is not `opencode/*`, which means allowing `x-opencode-tda` outside `providerID.startsWith("opencode")` — the edge of the 2026-09-08 three-layer header contract; or the observation is made without a live provider, by invoking the transform directly on a captured body inside the sandbox.
- Sandbox oracle so far: `_run.cmd models` exit 0 · phase 1 started the agent on the intended model and the pipeline reached the wire (the failure came back FROM the provider).

## 2026-09-19 — T5: the observation obtained WITHOUT the sandbox, and what a real body showed that a fixture could not

- **The sandbox still cannot be observed** (residual, unexplained): the runtime config is provably READ there (the model id resolves from it, and an unknown id produced `Model not found`), the gateway catalogue entry for the local double survived in `<sandbox>/gateway.jsonc`, and the double ANSWERS a direct probe (`status 200`, SSE) — yet **no request reaches it** and the run fails with `Response stream ended without a finish reason`. That message is identical for Zen and for the local double, which is the clue worth keeping: the failure is upstream-INDEPENDENT, so it is not the endpoint. One discriminator remains unrun: point the base URL at a CLOSED port — a connection error would prove the URL is consulted, the same message would prove no fetch happens at all.
- **The observation was obtained anyway, and this is the instrument to keep:** `experiments/2026-09-19_tda-sandbox/observe-real-body.ts` (run from the repo root) drives the REAL transform over a REAL captured request body.
- **Measured [Exact]**, on the newest media-bearing capture (`2026-09-19T22-42-52-626Z-…json`): 249 bodies scanned, 7 carry media; body **938 424 chars**; the block sits at `$.body.messages[99].content[1]`; payload `data:image/webp;base64,…` **143 chars**; digest `a3a4288f…`.
- **The differential [Exact]:** delta **+44**, first divergence at 789543, common tail 148705 — removed the WHOLE `"image_url","image_url":{"url":"data:…"}` block (176 chars), inserted the pointer (220 chars), **the rest of the 938 KB body byte-identical**, and the CONTROL (same set, unmatched digest) leaves it identical. §0.6-4's falsifier is therefore satisfied on real bytes, not on a fixture.
- **WHAT THE REAL BODY SHOWED THAT A FIXTURE COULD NOT** — the payload rides the request in MORE THAN ONE FORM: 6 raw occurrences; the structured `content[]` entry is rewritten (1), and FIVE others survive inside strings, named by position: `messages[101] role=tool`, `messages[102] role=assistant` (2× — prose quoting the payload), `messages[113] role=tool`, `messages[115] role=tool`. The transform rewrites the wire SHAPE and, by its own contract, does not touch text — so a "release" removes one occurrence and leaves the bytes on the wire as text. Several of these are MY OWN doing this session (I printed the payload while investigating — which is itself the hazard, recorded: quoting a payload into the transcript re-places it on the wire).
- **Two probe defects of mine, both caught by the instrument rather than by reasoning:** (1) the first run "proved" the transform does not withhold — because I passed the CAPTURE WRAPPER (`{path, headers, body}`) as if it were the body, and the transform answered exactly as rule 4 says it must (unparsable shape → return the input unchanged). A zero from a probe is not a fact until the probe is validated. (2) I reported "0 of 241 bodies carry media" and nearly filed it as a converter defect — the truth was that I had attached no image in that window; attaching one proved images DO reach the wire (`{type:"image_url", image_url:{url:"data:image/webp;base64,…"}}`).
- **Model correction from the owner:** `opencode/mimo-v2.5-free` is a TEXT model, so it cannot serve as a vision carrier. Moot for the local double (nothing real is called there), but it stands for any run that must actually see.
- **SCOPE CORRECTION on the finding above, made the moment it was measurable:** in THIS body all five surviving occurrences are echoes of my own investigation — `messages[115]`'s window shows a captured stdout of `observe-real-body.ts` itself (`first divergence: 789543 … removed (176 chars): "image_url\"…`). So the mechanism is real, but this instance is self-inflicted. The GENERAL path is any tool output that carries a data URL as text — a `dbread` of an image part's row, a `grep` that matched one, a captured body, a `recall` of such a result — none of which existed in this body.
- **Sizes, for the record:** 5 surviving occurrences × 143 chars = **715 chars** on the wire past a release. A real screenshot payload is ~100 000 chars each, so the same mechanism scales ≈700×.
- **CORRECTION of a claim I made in prose and never recorded here — and the measurement RETIRES the worry.** I said "the stored string and the wire string are not the same". Measured with ONE extractor on both ends (the previous probe used two and reported 0 against 8 in the same process — a probe defect, not a fact): **349** stored image payload urls, and **the wire url IS among them verbatim** (`one of them IS the wire url: true`; 143 chars, and 143 appears in the stored length set). So `normalize.ts`'s own claim is confirmed by measurement — «what is stored is what every later request sends» — and a digest taken from the committed part DOES match the wire. The "long nightmare with all the preprocessors" is therefore retired **for this path, by measurement rather than by argument**.
- What that does NOT retire, and why the wire still owns the truth: equality is measured for this provider and this shape (`image_url`), not guaranteed. A provider whose wire shape differs, a payload that has left the window, or any future step below the commit would each break it silently — which is why the transform must report what it withheld and a mismatch must speak. Range of stored payloads seen, for scale: 24 … 1 300 838 chars, several over 100 000 — real screenshots ride here.

## 2026-09-19 — TDA: the owner collapsed the design to a TABLE (message id → ttl); my T6 verdict work reverted

- Owner: «мы стрипаем мультимедиа сообщения сейчас… нам по сути не хватает просто маленькой таблички message id и ttl и всё, и вообще ничего городить не надо, тогда будет просто read file c ttl».
- **I checked the premise instead of agreeing with it — and it holds:** the strip is real and it lives at CONVERSION. A tool result heavier than `TOOL_PLACEHOLDER_THRESHOLD_CHARS` (8 000) is replaced on the wire by a placeholder STRING (`message-v2.ts:1124`) while the part stays intact in history, and media inside tool results is split/re-shaped at `:1246-1264` / `:1340-1367`. The `stripMedia` OPTION is gone — deleted today as dead (`:1149` records that nothing ever set it).
- ⇒ If a payload can leave at conversion, it never needed to reach the wire, and the gateway has nothing to do in this story: no digest, no header, no expectation, no verdict, no note. Cost is the SAME (bytes leave the middle of the body either way ⇒ the prefix changes once — the «частичная потеря кэша» the owner accepted when choosing the chain placement), and the effect is the same (history intact, only the request changes).
- **Reverted the unfinished T6** (three files: `tda.ts`, `acquired-item.ts`, `acquired-item-store.ts`) from session backups rather than finishing a design that had just been superseded — an incomplete contract change must not sit in the tree red. Green again: `typecheck` exit 0 · gateway-tda 9/0 · acquired-item 6/0 · acquired-item-store 4/0 · marker `REVERTED_GREEN`.
- The GATEWAY leg is **not** deleted: it is committed, green and tested, and the transition is problem-driven and gradual («мы ничего выкорчевывать не будем»). It retires when nothing uses it — not by an architecture decision.
- **Next:** the `(message/part id → ttl)` table, `read` with a TTL, and the strip branch at the SAME decision point that already decides the placeholder. Oracle is the instrument already in hand: a real capture plus the DB — «before the TTL the payload is in the body, after it is not, and the part is still in history».

## 2026-09-19 — the TTL table and the conversion gate shipped — and the gate is INERT by design

- Owner: «нам по сути не хватает просто маленькой таблички message id и ttl… тогда будет просто read file c ttl».
- **Table:** `held_media (message_id PK, session_id, expires_at_turn, reason, time_created, time_updated)` — DDL in `storage/db.ts` (idempotent, beside the precedent) and drizzle in `storage/schema-project.sql.ts`; access in `session/held-media.ts` (`holdMedia` upserts and EXTENDS rather than duplicating; `expiredMediaMessages`; `listHeldMedia`). The boundary is `expires_at_turn < turn` — the same direction as `isWithheld`, so the two sides cannot disagree about "spent".
- **Tests:** `test/session/held-media.test.ts` **4 pass / 0 fail / 11 expect** — including the PRAGMA oracle reconciling the DDL with the drizzle declaration (nothing else compares those two descriptions of one table), the turn boundary (a span ending ON this turn is still held), extension-instead-of-duplication, and per-session isolation.
- **The conversion gate:** in `message-v2.ts`, a copy of the messages whose held payload has been dropped (`file` part → a text note; a tool part's `state.attachments` → empty plus the note appended to the result text). ONE point, UPSTREAM of every site that can emit a payload, so no site can forget it.
- **A REGRESSION, caught by the proportional suite — and the best argument for running it:** the first version of the gate queried the DB for the span straight from the conversion, and **all 42 `message-v2` cases failed** with `No context found for database`, because the conversion is also called outside an Instance (unit tests, paths with no project DB). Redone: the CALLER supplies the set (`options.expiredMedia`), and this module holds no session and reads no database. The gate is therefore INERT by default — zero behaviour change until a caller passes the map.
- Oracle: `bun typecheck` exit 0 · `message-v2.test.ts` **42 pass / 0 fail / 72 expect** (unchanged count, which is the inertness proven) · `held-media.test.ts` 4/0/11 · `acquired-item*` 6/0 and 4/0 untouched.
- **Residual, and it is the NEXT BLOCK:** (a) `read` declares `holdTurns` and the runtime writes the row keyed by the message id it creates; (b) the caller passes `expiredMedia` into the conversion (`prompt.ts` / `llm.ts`, where an Instance exists); (c) the behaviour test — «before the TTL the payload is in the body, after it is not, and the part is still in history». **Without (b) the gate never fires at all** — recorded here so that "green" is not read as "working".

## 2026-09-19 — FOUND BY READING THE DATABASE: the turn counter read an EMPTY table, so no span would ever expire

- The owner's aside («если посмотреть базу там море таблиц с непонятными целями») produced an inventory, and the inventory produced a defect: **`session_entry` holds ZERO rows**, and `currentTurn` counted turns from exactly there (`type = 'user'`). A counter pinned at 0 makes `expires_at_turn < turn` never true, so **no span would ever expire** — true of the already-shipped `acquired_item` AND of the designed TTL, which both stand on this counter.
- Inventory (one SELECT over `sqlite_master` plus counts): `event` 87 548 · `part` 65 184 · `message` 15 368 · `balance_snapshot` 1 757 (grows like a log) · `todo` 116 · `project_checkpoint` 47 · `event_sequence` 26 · `session` 17 · `migration` 10 · `project` 1 · **and six at zero**: `session_entry`, `media_token_calibration`, `part_embedding`, `permission`, `session_share`, `workspace`.
- **A LIVE SOURCE FOUND AND MEASURED:** `message` — 15 369 total, **1 577 user**, 13 792 assistant. The role lives INSIDE the json `data` (there is no role column), hence `json_extract(data, '$.role') = 'user'`.
- Fixed: `currentTurn` re-based on `message`, and so is its own doc comment, which had asserted the untrue. The store suite's fixture moved from `session_entry` to `message` — **and that is exactly why the defect survived: the fixture and the code agreed with each other, and neither agreed with the database.**
- Oracles: `bun typecheck` exit 0 · `acquired-item-store` **4 pass / 0 fail / 17 expect** · live database: this session counts **335 turns** (0 on the old source), the busiest session 414.
- Lesson: zero rows in a source table reads as "the feature was never used" and actually means "nothing writes the source" ⇒ a mechanism that never fires while staying green. A fixture written by the author cannot see the author's defect. Ask the DATABASE.

## 2026-09-19 — the TTL migration: the field lives ON THE PART, and the oracle refuted a claim of my own

- Owner: «Дальше для ttl всетаки надо поле чтобы по нему независимо идти… перед началом реализации решить вопрос с остальными полями и подчистить — потому что это миграция чтобы разом, предложи оптимизацию чтобы работало быстро».
- The answer, grounded in the field survey: `ttl` becomes TWO columns on `part` — `ttl_until integer` (the RESOLVED absolute turn; NULL means the mechanism does not apply, so "permanent" needs no value of its own and an old row already reads as permanent) and `ttl_scope text` (`tmp_xxx`). Absolute rather than "turns remaining" IS the optimisation asked for: the reader does a numeric range, never per-row arithmetic. Index `(session_id, ttl_until)`, so the lookup is this session's parts in expiry order.
- The precedent for the shape already existed and did not have to be invented: `compacted`, «promoted from JSON data.compacted for indexable visible loads», reversible by `revert`, migration `20260601000002_message_compacted_column`.
- Shipped: `migration/20260919000000_part_ttl_columns.ts` (a real `PRAGMA` guard on the columns rather than a blind try/catch, and an early return when `part` is absent — **migrations run BEFORE the core schema DDL**, `db.ts:76` then `:81`), the columns in the `db.ts` DDL for a fresh database, and the columns in the LIVE declaration `session/session.sql.ts`. Also the message role index for the turn counter.
- Found while grounding, and it is the "two descriptions of one table" class again: `storage/schema-project.sql.ts` declares `PartTable` a SECOND time WITHOUT the `type`/`tool_name`/`status` columns that migration `20260730` added — a stale duplicate that only the uncommitted gateway leg imports. Next cleanup candidate.
- **Oracle, and it is an artifact oracle** (`.opencode/migration-check.mjs`, run against a COPY of the live database): both columns present, both indexes present, a second run is a no-op (the guard holds), data untouched — 65 458 parts before and after — and the counter's own query plan is `SEARCH message USING COVERING INDEX message_session_role_idx`, so the new index is actually used. Plus `bun typecheck` exit 0 and `turn + message-v2` 44 pass / 0 fail / 80 expect, where every test instance runs the whole migration chain on a fresh schema.
- That oracle is now COMMITTED as a test rather than left as a script: `test/storage/migration-part-ttl.test.ts` builds the previous schema by hand and asserts the three failure modes that matter — a second run is a no-op (a bad guard would crash the database at boot), a database with no `part` is left alone, and existing rows survive with NULL ttl (which is the wanted semantics).
- **REFUTED, MY OWN CLAIM:** I had written that `held_media` "was physically created". The live database has NO such table — the DDL that created it only ever ran inside tmpdir instances, because no runtime carrying it was ever built. The oracle caught it by demanding a starting state that does not exist. `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS held_media` stays, but it is DEFENSIVE, and the migration comment now says so. Same lesson as the rest of the day: read the state, never carry yesterday's guess forward as today's fact.

## 2026-09-19 — the ttl field rides in the part's json, and the columns are DERIVED at the write

- The shape is taken from `compacted` rather than invented: the value lives in the json `data`, and the column is a derivative for indexable/queryable loads, produced by the ONE write point (`projectors.ts`, the `PartUpdated` projection).
- `partBase` (`message-v2.ts:100`) is the shared base of EVERY part type, so the two fields go there — and `PartData`, the type of the json column, is derived from that type, so nothing has to copy them by hand.
- `ttlUntil` is an ABSOLUTE turn, not "turns remaining": a relative value does not say from WHEN, and the reader that has to judge it is the conversion, which sees stored parts long after the tool that made them. Absent means the mechanism does not apply, so "permanent" needs no value of its own.
- The projector derives `ttl_until`/`ttl_scope` from the same `rest` it stores as json, so the two homes of one value stay together because ONE writer owns both — and it needs no `as any`, because the field is on the base and is therefore typed on every variant.
- Found while grounding: `storage/schema-project.sql.ts` declares these core tables a SECOND time (stale — its `PartTable` lacks the `type`/`tool_name`/`status` columns migration `20260730` added). Only the uncommitted gateway leg imports that file. Next cleanup candidate, recorded in the database-truth plan.
- Oracle (`test/session/part-ttl-storage.test.ts`, new): undeclared ⇒ NULL on BOTH sides; declared ⇒ present in the json AND the columns; moved ⇒ both move; dropped ⇒ both clear. Plus `bun typecheck` exit 0 and `part-ttl-storage + message-v2` 43 pass / 0 fail / 76 expect.
- Residual of this unit, stated so green is not read as working: the conversion gate STILL reads the old `options.expiredMedia` map, so nothing yet consults the part's own field. Next: switch the gate to the part's field against ONE number (the current turn), threaded through `toolReplayOptions` — and the behaviour test where a payload leaves the wire while the part stays in history.

## 2026-09-19 — the conversion gate now judges the PART's own field, and the release carries an address

- `dropExpiredMedia` (a map of message ids, appended a note, left the output in place) is replaced by `releaseExpiredParts`: the judgement is per PART, from the part's own `ttlUntil` against ONE number the caller supplies, and the payload is REPLACED, not annotated — "the piece disappears, the message stays", which is the owner's own wording for this mechanism.
- A released tool result keeps a note that NAMES ITS ID. That is not politeness: `recall` fetches by part id, and the stored part still holds the full output, so the note is a real address — the release is on the WIRE, never in the record. A file part's note says to read the file again, which is that case's honest address.
- `ttlScope` (`tmp_xxx`) is deliberately NOT judged: nothing yet says whether a temporary enable is alive, and a guess would either release what is held or hold what was released. An unjudged scope rides and says nothing. Same reasoning as the earlier `expectedOnWire` rule.
- An absent `turn` means NOT JUDGED, never "expired" — the direction of that mistake empties a result the model still needed.
- **A second "one thing described N times" defect found by the compiler:** the conversion's options type was written out THREE times (the effect and both wrappers), so a new field reached only the copy that happened to be edited. Now ONE exported `ConversionOptions` is referenced by all three. The compiler caught it only because a test passed `turn` to a wrapper that did not declare it.
- Oracle, `test/session/part-ttl-gate.test.ts` (new, pure unit, no DB): no span and a running span both RIDE; a span ending exactly ON this turn is still held (same direction as the store's `expires_at_turn < turn`); a spent span releases the payload AND the wire names the part id and `recall`; the source part is unchanged afterwards (release on the wire, not in the record); an errored result releases the same way; no turn judges nothing. Plus `bun typecheck` exit 0 and `part-ttl-gate + part-ttl-storage + message-v2` 48 pass / 0 fail / 88 expect.
- Residual, so green is not read as working: **nothing in production passes `turn` yet**, so the gate is inert on the wire. The wiring is small but touches the send path — the real sites are the ones passing `lastUser?.id` (not `prompt.ts:345`, which is title generation), and they must pass `currentTurn(lastUser.sessionID)`.

## 2026-09-19 — the turn reaches EVERY conversion of session history; and a negative control killed my own justification

- `toolReplayOptions(cfg, afterMessageID, sessionID)` — the third argument is required and the current turn is derived from the session INSIDE one resolver, not repeated as an expression at every call site that would have to remember it. Both arguments are required on purpose: `undefined` is a DECISION stated at the call, and a site added later cannot inherit "no release" by forgetting. The compiler asks at every one of them.
- EIGHT sites are wired: the three request-assembly branches (which also splice a prefix out of the checkpoint), `captureSummary`'s checkpoint, the two turn-checkpoint saves, and the summary path. The checkpoint paths MUST see the same turn, because a checkpoint has to store what the trunk sends — one built without it froze full tool outputs the trunk had already collapsed (prefix divergence from message 9, 541_502 tokens recomputed, 2026-09-07). The single deliberate exception is title generation: it names the session's opening messages rather than delivering history to the main model, and runs where no declared span can have passed.
- The compile oracle caught that `captureSummary`'s identifier is `input.sessionID`, not `sessionID` — a scope error that would otherwise have shipped silently, since a plain name lookup was the only thing distinguishing them.
- **MY JUSTIFICATION ABOUT THE CACHE WAS FALSE, and a negative control is what killed it.** I added a `turn` component to the conversion cache key, claiming that without it "the release would silently never fire". The negative control — remove the component and re-run — stayed GREEN, so the claim was false. The reason is structural: a release REWRITES the parts, and the cache fingerprint `contentFp` is taken FROM those parts, so a pre-release entry and a released one cannot share a key at any turn. The component was removed, the comment rewritten to the measured argument, and the test renamed to the property it actually verifies ("a cached pre-release entry is never served after the release").
- Oracles: `bun typecheck` exit 0 · `part-ttl-gate + part-ttl-storage + message-v2` **49 pass / 0 fail / 91 expect** · `prompt.test.ts` **42 pass / 13 skip / 0 fail / 169 expect** — exactly the recorded baseline.
- Residual, stated so green is not read as working: the gate is now LIVE on the wire wherever a part carries `ttlUntil`, but **nothing writes that field yet outside the tests** — `TempEnable` / `TempDisable` are what put a span on a part, and they are the next unit.

## 2026-09-19 — the WRITERS exist (`tempenable` / `tempdisable`), and the mechanism is usable end to end

- Two tools in one module (`tool/temp-lifetime.ts`): `tempenable(id, turns?, scope?, reason)` puts a declared span on a part, `tempdisable(id, reason)` removes it. Both return the shape the owner specified — `id`, `report`, `ids_range` — and echo the reason into their own output, so the motive is readable after the fact. The address is the part id a placeholder prints, so no new address surface is invented: a piece whose id the model has never seen is a piece it cannot name.
- **CORRECTED — the fault was mine twice over, and the rule was already on this page.** I first wrote this as "the runtime refused the owner's capitalization AND the underscore". The owner had written `TempEnable` / `TempDisable`, WITHOUT an underscore: `temp_enable` was MY invention, so that sentence blamed him for my own form. He then pointed out that `_` and `-` are refused for tool names — and the rule was **already recorded in this very journal, 2026-08-26**: «tool names in kernel texts normalized to wire ids (`canonicalName` strips all non-alphanumerics; models confuse `-`/`_`): … multi_edit→multiedit …». I had used `multiedit` all session — the rule's own example — and still authored an underscore name, then reported the refusal as somebody else's doing. Retracted. The names are `tempenable` / `tempdisable`, renamed everywhere the name is recognised (registry import, service, map, builtin list, `dsml-normalizer`, without which a DeepSeek-emitted call is never recognised). The durable product is NOT the rename: it is reading the wire-name rule BEFORE authoring a tool, and never attributing a form I authored to the owner.
- **DEFECT FOUND WHILE WRITING, and it would have inverted the contract:** `releaseExpiredParts` tested `ttlUntil !== undefined`, so a `null` span — which the contract ITSELF defines as permanent — coerced to `0` and read as EXPIRED. `tempdisable` writes exactly that value, so it would have released the piece it was told to keep. The gate now reads the NUMBER (`typeof part.ttlUntil === "number"`), with a new case pinning `null` ⇒ rides. Same trap as this morning's migration guard: bun's `get()` answers null, not undefined.
- "Permanent" is expressed by OMITTING the keys rather than storing `null`: both read as SQL NULL, but only one leaves the stored json clean and nothing can later mistake a sentinel for a live span.
- The part lookup is now ONE (`session/stored-part.ts`, `readStoredPart`) and `recall` goes through it too — two lookups would be two places that must remember to read the identity from the table COLUMNS, and that omission is exactly what made `keep` persist nothing in every build.
- Oracles: `bun typecheck` exit 0 · the new `test/tool/temp-lifetime.test.ts` (3 cases: span arithmetic including its boundary, negatives and fractions; the omit-don't-sentinel representation; and the write **end to end against a real database** — the declaration read back from the COLUMNS, the payload gone from the wire one turn past the span with the part id kept as the address, and the payload back once the declaration is removed) · `recall + gate + storage + message-v2 + temp-lifetime` **73 pass / 0 fail / 169 expect**.
- Residual, stated so green is not read as working: `scope` (`tmp_xxx`) is stored and reported but **not judged** — nothing yet says whether a named temporary enable is still alive, and a guess would either release what is held or hold what was released. The named-toggle form (enable a scope, have what is acquired bind to it, release the group) needs a home for that toggle's state — a key-namespace decision, not a code one. Next units: the fold auto-release (reset at compaction, message kept), and telling the tools which pieces are holdable.

## 2026-09-19 — the fold's hard release: the last piece of T6's contract

- At the fold boundary — the SAME seam as `AcquiredSet.releaseAll` (`prompt.ts`) — every still-running span is moved into the PAST: `ttlUntil = turn - 1`. **Expired, never un-declared**: clearing the declaration would make the piece PERMANENT again and put its payload straight back on the wire, i.e. the exact opposite of a reset. A value one turn in the past makes the release bite IN THIS TURN rather than the next.
- The reset walks the ALREADY-LOADED window (`visibleAfter`) instead of querying: those parts are in hand, each carries its own declaration, and a piece outside the window is not being sent. So the `(session_id, ttl_until)` index has no reader at this site — its reader will be the listing of what is holdable (the next unit). Recorded here so the index does not read as forgotten.
- The predicates were extracted to ONE place (`declaredUntil`, `isSpanExpired`, `expiredSpan`, `spansToExpire` in `message-v2.ts`) and are used by BOTH readers — the wire gate and the fold reset. A one-turn disagreement between them would be invisible on both sides; now the comparison is single by construction.
- The reset RETURNS what it releases and the caller logs count + ids: a release nobody can enumerate is indistinguishable from a loss.
- **Honest residual, recorded rather than hidden:** this turn's summary is captured BEFORE the fold is decided (`captureSidecar` at 2689, `foldDecision` at 2707), so a span still live at that instant appears in it as live; the reset turns it into a pointer from the next request on. The strict reading of «в summary не считается» needs the fold to be known BEFORE the summary is taken — a re-ordering of the seam, not a two-line change.
- Oracles: `bun typecheck` exit 0 · `part-ttl-gate + temp-lifetime + recall + part-ttl-storage + message-v2` **74 pass / 0 fail / 175 expect** · `prompt.test.ts` **42 pass / 13 skip / 0 fail / 169 expect**, exactly the recorded baseline.
- **Falsifier of this change, and it bit TWICE in one session:** inserting a test while anchoring on the closing braces TRUNCATES the body of the test above it. `createChunk`-style appends must anchor on real content ABOVE the insertion point, and the file must be read back afterwards — the tool's own success report is not evidence. The first instance was caught by `bun typecheck`, the second only by reading the file; both times the lost body was already gone.

## 2026-09-20 — eyes on `/agents` at last, and the pipeline test's recipe

- I have a TUI of my own now — `cmd_runner start --terminal wt --direct-terminal --auto-tail 0 --cols 110 --rows 34 -- bin\opencode.exe`, run `20260920T010405Z_09332e87`, `/agents` opened through the inbox — so the form is judged from PIXELS, which is exactly what the earlier blind edits lacked. Capture: `.opencode/shots/mine-agents.png`; boot check: `.opencode/shots/mine-boot.png`.
- **The form's defects, read off my own capture** (all four visible at `medium`, 60 wide inside a 110-column terminal):
  1. the value column is CLIPPED at the dialog's right edge mid-word, with no ellipsis (`huggingface/zai-org/GLM-5.3-`);
  2. the description line is clipped at that same edge instead of WRAPPING (`Autonomous development orchestrator (orchestrator_ag`);
  3. the state markers are jammed against the name (`●✓orchestrator_agent`, no gap);
  4. the dialog occupies roughly 55% of the terminal while its own content is cut.
- The cause class is already recorded from the earlier dialog work: **in a flex column a child gets NO explicit width from its parent**, so `space-between` / `flex-end` silently degrade. The HEADER was fixed that way (`72ce065a0e`); the ROW columns were not. So the fix is explicit column widths plus `wrapMode` on the row texts — NOT the dialog size: `8c9a8aefb1` (xlarge) was REVERTED by `59d0a0bc90` because "a wide dialog breaks the row columns", i.e. widening moved the clipping instead of removing it. That revert is the record of the blind-first approach the owner named («ты сломал эту форму потому что у тебя не было видения»).
- Observed difference, NOT explained: in my fresh session the rows show real models (`openai/gpt-5.6-sol ← active`, `huggingface/zai-org/GLM-5.3-…`) while the owner's session shows `inherits from worktree` for the same agents. Same binary, different session layer.
- **The pipeline test's recipe** (the session must be younger than the tools; THIS one is not — the provider tool catalog is fixed at session start, so `tempenable` is absent from it even though it is in the binary):
  1. a session started AFTER the rebuild, so `tempenable` / `tempdisable` are in its catalog;
  2. a turn whose result is heavy enough for the delivered-once placeholder to print `id=prt_…`;
  3. `tempenable(id=…, turns=0, reason=…)` — `turns: 0` resolves to THIS turn, so the release must bite in the very next request;
  4. read the captured body under `.opencode/data/gateway/raw-wire/` for that next request: the payload must be GONE and `[held] payload released … recall with id=…` must stand in its place — the two mechanisms print DIFFERENT strings, so the note is distinguishable from the placeholder;
  5. `tempdisable(id=…)` and re-read: the payload returns.
- Model choice matters for that test: a fresh session defaults to `openai/gpt-5.6-sol` (paid), so the run must name a free model explicitly.

## 2026-09-20 — the /agents row clipping is fixed WITH eyes: the row had no width, so it resolved to content size

- **Shipped** in `packages/opencode/src/cli/cmd/tui/ui/dialog-select.tsx`, ONE place (the shared `DialogSelect`), so every dialog whose rows come from it inherits the fix. Commit follows this entry.
- The four defects were measured from MY OWN capture (`.opencode/shots/mine-agents.png`, run `20260920T010405Z_09332e87`) and are the same class as the header fix `72ce065a0e`: **in a flex column a child gets no width from its parent**, so the row resolved to CONTENT size, overran the dialog, and the last columns were cut mid-word with no ellipsis. Two further causes, both in code: `Locale.truncate(props.title, 61)` was a HARD 61 for every dialog size, and the footer/description carried `wrapMode="none" overflow="hidden"` with no ellipsis of their own. The marker overlap (`●✓orchestrator_agent`) was the ABSOLUTE `[✓]` at left 1 sharing columns with the gutter whenever `paddingLeft` was 1.
- The fix: `rowTextWidth = rowWidth() − 16` (the worst case — marker + gutter + paddings + gaps), the row states `width={rowWidth() − 2}` so the flex chain is determinate, `paddingLeft` reserves 5 when a marker renders (3 when only a gutter, 1 when the row IS current), and ONE budget table truncates the three fields — the footer yields first and never takes more than half the row, the name keeps its `MIN_TITLE_WIDTH` floor.
- **Oracle: a render, from my own TUI** — a before/after pair on a freshly built `dist/bin`: `mine-agents.png` → `fixed-agents.png`. Fixed by the pair: the value column now elides (`huggingface/zai-org/G…`), the description elides (`…Full tool…`), marker and gutter are separated (`[✓] ● orchestrator_agent`), and the value column is aligned across rows. `bun typecheck` exit 0, read from `state.json` (01:21:13→01:21:27), never inferred from silence.
- **Not changed, and it is a decision rather than a defect:** the dialog is `medium` (60) inside a 110-column terminal. The rows now FIT their box, which means the earlier `xlarge` attempt — reverted by `59d0a0bc90` with "a wide dialog breaks the row columns" — was fighting the missing width, not the width itself. Widening is therefore safe to re-try, by the owner's call.
- **Method note, because this form was broken once already:** the earlier attempt was made from CODE (`8c9a8aefb1`, reverted) and this one from PIXELS — the same screenshot that found the defects is what accepted the fix. One round: launch, one sequenced send+capture, one read. No key-thrash loop.
- Residual: the fix lives in `dist/`, NOT deployed to `bin/` (the owner's action). The sibling dialogs that build their OWN header row (confirm / alert / prompt / export-options / tui-config) still carry the header-width defect, though their ROWS now come from the fixed component.

## 2026-09-20 — the memory spine: the session's own memory became navigable

- **Shipped** `c96b485a84` — `feat(memory): the session memory spine - epochs, the descent, and the address between them` (6 files, +685/−1): `src/memory/spine.ts` (pure), `Memory.listDominants` in `memory/memory.ts`, the summaries corpus + the `dominants` descent in `tool/messagesearch.ts` (+ its `DESCRIPTION`), `test/memory/spine.test.ts`, `plans/2026-09-20_memory-spine-navigation.md`.
- Three levels over columns that already existed: `{ corpus: "summaries" }` = the spine, one line per epoch; `{ corpus: "summaries", epoch: N }` = that epoch's body; `{ corpus: "parts", dominants: true, range: "msg_a..msg_b" }` = the messages of that range with their own dominants. Plus `dominant:` (filter), `goals: true`, `last: N`. The existing accessor `IncrementalCheckpoint.listAll` is reused — no raw SQL in the tool.
- **Why it matters, measured:** 43 epochs, `dominant_present 43/43`, `goal_present 43/43`, **7 040 chars** of spine against **634 525 chars** of bodies (90:1), and against ~100 k tokens for ONE raw `m*` snapshot.
- **Oracles:** `bun typecheck` exit 0 (a baseline was captured BEFORE the edit, also exit 0); `spine.test.ts` **14 pass / 0 fail / 18 expect**; live over all 43 real bodies (`experiments/2026-09-20_memory-spine/spine-live.ts` + `.out.txt`); live descent driving the REAL functions rather than a copy of the query (`dominants-live.ts`) — **8 carriers** in the last epoch's range, **245** session-wide, 36 ms / 45 ms, whole run including a 1 530-part sync = **2 s** wall.
- **Two extraction rules, each measured — do not unify them:** `extractDominant(body)` takes the FIRST marker (an epoch body carries its vector block at the head); `extractMessageDominant(text)` takes the LAST (an answer QUOTES other dominants; its own vector is written last). They differ on 3 of 197 parts. The message corpus also needs two exclusions, both measured: 17 `=== COMPACTED ===` snapshots carry the dominants of EVERY summary, and a tool output quotes what it read (marker 81 311 chars from the end).
- **A range comes first because it is the cheap path, and that is a number:** the same marker filter costs **1 563 ms** over the whole index against **15 ms** for a range query.
- **Falsifier found by the instrument's own first run:** `carriers=0` in range while session-wide gave 197. Cause read from state, not guessed — the boundary ids were ABSENT from `part_index`, which held **3 539 of the session's 3 885 messages**: the index is synced LAZILY and only the tool does it (`messagesearch.ts:78`). An instrument that calls `listDominants` directly measures the LAG, not the query; a zero from an unvalidated probe is not a fact.
- **The trap I walked into again:** `ExecuteResult<M>` infers M from EVERY branch of `execute`, so one tool may not return two `metadata` key sets → `TYPECHECK_EXIT=2`. Already recorded from `recall` and not read. The fix follows the repo precedent: one shape (`{query, mode, results}`) on every return, with the address in the output text where it is read.
- Method that held: `message_id` ordering was verified with a positive control (**0 inversions**, control **3 510/3 510**) BEFORE any lexicographic range was relied on; and the first instrument's implausible zero was chased by reading state rather than by re-running it harder.

## 2026-09-20 — the memory carrier gets a budget that reports and never acts

- **Shipped** `70b4281eba` — `feat(memory): the carrier's budget is a flag, not an actor` (5 files,
  +236/−27): `src/memory/budget.ts` (pure: `MEMORY_TOKENS_LIMIT = 96_000`, `memoryFlag`,
  `oldestEntryDates`), wired into the **`append`/`write` outputs** of `src/tool/memory.ts`, plus
  `test/memory/budget.test.ts` and one deliberately updated assertion in `test/tool/memory.test.ts`.
- **The measurement that started it (T-M1):** the carrier is **73 345 tokens — 7.6 % of the 957 232 fold
  budget**, taken with the fold's OWN counter, `countTokens` (never a ratio). Of four instruments the WORST
  was **the one we use to budget the PROMPT** (≈ −2.7×): a different SPACE, i.e. the "two measures under one
  name" defect again. Evidence: `experiments/2026-09-20_memory-budget/`.
- **The owner's objection IS the design.** An earlier version of this task proposed a declared limit that
  automatically ROTATES the oldest entries out. Owner: «Стоп, ты хочешь чтобы твоя память ротовалась сама
  собой?» — and the measurement backed him: of 82 entries only **8 carry `Status: ACTIVE`**, and the OLDEST
  of the rest is the *misattribution-reflex* criterion, the most ALIVE thing in the file, so oldest-first
  would retire it FIRST. **A threshold deciding what the agent forgets is a decision taken by nobody** —
  the silent class this project hunts. So the limit is a FLAG and never an actor; retirement is a
  deliberate act that leaves a one-line pointer.
- **Placement, and why NOT `read`:** the flag rides the mutation outputs, because the four `read`
  assertions pin the passthrough contract (`output` IS the file) — and, more importantly, a caller echoing
  a read back into `write` would copy the flag INTO the carrier. Growth happens on mutation, so that is
  where the actor sees the number. Above **80 %** the flag names the oldest dates and says who decides;
  below it, it is a number. Live over the real carrier: `Memory: 73,345 tokens of 96,000 (76.4 %).`
- **FINDING that limits T-M6, and it is a SURFACE problem, not a policy one:** «merge-on-append» **cannot
  be performed with today's tool surface at this size** — `append` only appends, and merging a dated entry
  would mean rewriting the whole ~250 KB carrier through `write`, which cannot be reproduced by hand and is
  priced in output tokens. So growth is FORCED by the surface. The realistic deliberate act when the flag
  fires is a consolidation `write` (the previous content is kept in `revisions/`), or a small bounded tool
  addition: an action that replaces ONE dated entry. Recorded, not taken.
- Oracles: `bun typecheck` exit 0 (a baseline was taken BEFORE the edit, also 0); `test/memory/budget.test.ts`
  + `test/tool/memory.test.ts` **11 pass / 0 fail / 23 expect**; the live flag over the real carrier.
- Residual: nothing is live until the owner rebuilds the binary. Open owner decisions: **H3** (persist the
  claim ledger or declare its volatility) and the kernel ceiling for the four addon bindings.

## [2026-09-22 22:40] July-ABI imports land, funded entirely by removed prose

Task: pull what is still load-bearing from `opencode_prompts_kernel.txt` (the July BGE-era
ABI), place it by gate, and find the excess prose to pay for it.

Landed in `source.py` — twelve norms, none of them host-specific:
- G1 `@INSTRUMENT_ORDER` — try instruments by decisiveness per call; descend only on a
  recorded empty/failure; escalate the whole chain before saying "not found".
- G1 `@LOUD_FAILURE` — between two instruments prefer the one whose failure is VISIBLE. A
  scanner returns matches and so looks successful while missing dynamic dispatch,
  re-exports and runtime binding. This is the grep-instead-of-codegraph defect stated as a
  norm rather than as a tool preference.
- G1 `@DEVICE_STATE` — device/hardware state is observed, never recalled; it drifts across
  a fold. (The rule whose absence produced the CPU-pinned embedding run on 2026-09-22.)
- G0 `@STATE_FIRST` — publish the state before reasoning from it.
- G4 `@CONCRETE_BOUNDS` — every bound is an integer; a budget that cannot be exceeded
  cannot detect a STALL.
- G7 `@VERIFY_BEFORE_REDUCING`, `@NO_INVENTED_CONSTANTS`, `@ONE_TASK_OPEN`.
- G8 `@SIGNAL_CARDINALITY` — identical diagnostics from one source are ONE signal.
- `@INFORMATION_STATUS` — your own recall is the weakest rung and never evidence alone.
- `INTENTION_RESET` — a persisted criterion never restates this protocol: a rule already in
  the prefix is paid again verbatim in the fold and, once it drifts, competes with the
  original as a second authority (owner, 2026-09-22).
- G6 — the impact query runs for EVERY mutation binding: whether a surface has other
  consumers is its answer, not its precondition. (The old rule was circular.)

Per host: `INSTRUMENT_CHAIN` names the actual rungs, and the two hosts without a history
search say so instead of skipping the rung silently.

## [2026-09-23 01:10] @PREDICATE_POWER — the third way to be green and wrong

Source: an agent's own TOOL_HEALTH report from the TUI work, quoted by the owner. Two
instruments recorded as classes: `CodeRenderable` must be mounted or the frame recorder
shows only empty frames (61 sites), and — the load-bearing one — the "frame is not empty"
predicate does not distinguish the two branches it was comparing: with
`drawUnstyledText === false` the frames are non-empty too (measured: expected false, got
true). The mutation refuted the PREDICATE, not the code, and the report named the
replacement (read the text via `captureFrame()`, or count parses).

The gap that closes: G8 already required an instrument able to fail, and G1 now requires the
instrument to sit on the layer the claim lives on. Neither covers an instrument that is on
the right layer and still cannot tell the hypothesis from its alternative — a test with no
power. `@PREDICATE_POWER`: "The predicate is part of the instrument: it must separate the
hypothesis from its alternative, not merely return a value. If both branches satisfy it the
oracle has no power and its PASS closes as Unknown."

This completes a triple, one example each from the owner's field material:
- violated CONSTRAINT — an odometer wheel drawn not pressed to the pipe wall (the distance
  axis of the whole inspection is void, and no spec check sees it);
- violated EXTENT — an anomaly distribution chart spanning metres for a 120 km line, built
  from the canonical form of such a chart rather than from the data;
- zero DISCRIMINATING POWER — "frame is not empty" satisfied by both branches.

Funded from prose, again without moving the ceiling: a metaphor in `@INFORMATION_STATUS`, a
reason that restated its own requirement in `@DELEGATION_BINDING` and `@SELF_DIVERGENCE`, an
example catalogue in `@LOUD_FAILURE`, an emphasis clause in `@EVIDENCE_BOUNDED_CLOSURE`, and
a flourish in the new rule itself.

Oracle [Exact]:
- product 45 996 / 46 000 B (4 free), claude 45 673, codex 45 322; validator clean, dedup
  clean on all three.
- `python -m pytest prompt_kernel/tests/ -q` -> 106 passed, 0 failed.

  `18f80bf9728931522ae38cd0bcff9aaea5d2fe89fe5ab5efc877aec2fba232ed`, baseline repinned
  `35401ae7057c92cb…` -> `18f80bf972893152…`, `assert_current_kernel_unchanged()` clean.
